## User

Create or update `AGENTS.md` for this repository.

The goal is a compact instruction file that helps future OpenCode sessions avoid mistakes and ramp up quickly. Every line should answer: "Would an agent likely miss this without help?" If not, leave it out.

User-provided focus or constraints (honor these):


## How to investigate

Read the highest-value sources first:
- `README*`, root manifests, workspace config, lockfiles
- build, test, lint, formatter, typecheck, and codegen config
- CI workflows and pre-commit / task runner config
- existing instruction files (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.cursor/rules/`, `.cursorrules`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`)
- repo-local OpenCode config such as `opencode.json`

If architecture is still unclear after reading config and docs, inspect a small number of representative code files to find the real entrypoints, package boundaries, and execution flow. Prefer reading the files that explain how the system is wired together over random leaf files.

Prefer executable sources of truth over prose. If docs conflict with config or scripts, trust the executable source and only keep what you can verify.

## What to extract

Look for the highest-signal facts for an agent working in this repo:
- exact developer commands, especially non-obvious ones
- how to run a single test, a single package, or a focused verification step
- required command order when it matters, such as `lint -> typecheck -> test`
- monorepo or multi-package boundaries, ownership of major directories, and the real app/library entrypoints
- framework or toolchain quirks: generated code, migrations, codegen, build artifacts, special env loading, dev servers, infra deploy flow
- testing quirks: fixtures, integration test prerequisites, snapshot workflows, required services, flaky or expensive suites
- important constraints from existing instruction files worth preserving

Good `AGENTS.md` content is usually hard-earned context that took reading multiple files to infer.

## Questions

Only ask the user questions if the repo cannot answer something important. Use the `question` tool for one short batch at most.

Good questions:
- undocumented team conventions
- branch / PR / release expectations
- missing setup or test prerequisites that are known but not written down

Do not ask about anything the repo already makes clear.

## Writing rules

Include only high-signal, repo-specific guidance such as:
- exact commands and shortcuts the agent would otherwise guess wrong
- architecture notes that are not obvious from filenames
- conventions that differ from language or framework defaults
- setup requirements, environment quirks, and operational gotchas
- references to existing instruction sources that matter

Exclude:
- generic software advice
- long tutorials or exhaustive file trees
- obvious language conventions
- speculative claims or anything you could not verify
- content better stored in another file referenced via `opencode.json` `instructions`

When in doubt, omit.

Prefer short sections and bullets. If the repo is simple, keep the file simple. If the repo is large, summarize the few structural facts that actually change how an agent should work.

If `AGENTS.md` already exists at `/workspaces/420-5G4-ML`, improve it in place rather than rewriting blindly. Preserve verified useful guidance, delete fluff or stale claims, and reconcile it with the current codebase.

def load_and_preprocess_image(image_path, target_size=28, content_size=20):
    """
    Load an image, convert to grayscale, and preprocess for model input.
    
    Args:
        image_path: Path to the input image
        target_size: Size of the output square image (default: 28x28)
        content_size: Size of the resized content within the target (default: 20)

        numpy.ndarray: Preprocessed image array ready for 
-- code

probabilities = model.predict(image_array)[0]
predicted_class = np.argmax(probabilities)

print(f"Selon moi l'image est : {classes[predicted_class]}\n")

for i, class_name in enumerate(classes):
    print(f"{class_name}: {probabilities[i] * 100:.2f}%")

def rechercheInformationsPokemon(numPokemon, Pokedex):
    infosPokemon = []
    for pokemon in Pokedex:
        if(int(pokemon[0])==numPokemon):
            infosPokemon=[pokemon[0],pokemon[1],pokemon[4],pokemon[5],pokemon[6],pokemon[7],pokemon[8],pokemon[9],pokemon[10]]
            break
    return infosPokemon

Let's check actual file bytes: read output showed normal. `git diff` showed `-` LF then `+` CRLF, so working tree is CRLF for those. Could mention? It's noisy for agent when diffing. But is it repo-specific? Yes, checked-out files use CRLF (likely core.autocrlf=true) while devcontainer sets `files.eol: "\n"`. Editing a file could produce huge diffs. This is a real gotcha but maybe too niche. Let's verify with `git config --get core.autocrlf` output was blank? The shell output began with "agent/..." meaning core.autocrlf empty. Hmm. Let's check file command absence. Use python to inspect a file's line endings.

\ No newline at end of file
+
+FROM mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/cpp:3-debian13
+
+ARG REINSTALL_CMAKE_VERSION=3.22.2
+
+# Install C++ and Python development dependencies
+RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y \
+    build-essential \
+    gcc \
+    g++ \
+    gdb \
+    ninja-build \
+    git \
+    curl \
+    wget \
+    pkg-config \
+    python3 \
+    python3-pip \
+    python3-venv \
+    python3-dev \
+    python3-tk \
+    python3-pyqt5 \
+    x11-apps \
+    x11-utils \
+    x11-xserver-utils \
+    libx11-6 \
+    libxext6 \
+    libxrender1 \
+    libxtst6 \
+    libxi6 \
+    libgl1 \
+    libglib2.0-0 \
+    libegl1 \
+    libsm6 \
+    libice6 \
+    libxcb-xinerama0 \
+    mesa-utils \
+    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
+
+# Install selected CMake version
+COPY reinstall-cmake.sh /tmp/reinstall-cmake.sh
+
+RUN chmod +x /tmp/reinstall-cmake.sh \
+    && /tmp/reinstall-cmake.sh ${REINSTALL_CMAKE_VERSION} \
+    && rm -f /tmp/reinstall-cmake.sh
+
+# Install OpenCV and Python scientific dependencies
+RUN python3 -m venv /opt/venv --system-site-packages
+
+ENV PATH="/opt/venv/bin:${PATH}"
+
+RUN pip install --no-cache-dir \
+    numpy \
+    matplotlib \
+    opencv-python \
+    pandas \
+    scipy \
+    scikit-learn \
+    jupyter \
+    pyqt5
+
+# Ensure GUI environment is available
+ENV QT_X11_NO_MITSHM=1
+
+WORKDIR /workspaces
+
+USER vscode

# AGENTS.md (repo instructions)

## What this repo is
- Course assignment (Atelier ML, student Zachary Lewis). Three independent parts, no shared package/workspace:
  - `apprentissage/` — Python notebooks for Pokémon combat analysis/regression; model artifact `modele/modele_pokemon.mod`.
  - `analyse_image/` — Python notebooks for Fashion-MNIST CNN classification; model JSON + H5 weights.
  - `agent/` — self-contained C++17 CLI library manager (`bibliotheque`), CMake, devcontainer.

## Commands
C++:
```
cd agent && mkdir -p build && cd build && cmake .. && make
./bibliotheque -d ../data
```
CMake globs `*.cpp` in `agent/` (not recursive); new `.cpp` requires re-running cmake.
- Requires CMake >=3.21, C++17; build uses -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic.
- Always pass `-d <dir>` containing `books.txt` and `users.txt`; without it no file I/O and menu runs in-memory only. Sample data is `agent/data`.
- no tests/lint/CI. Verification = clean build + manual CLI interaction.

Python:
- No venv in repo usable; `analyse_image/.venv/` is Windows-format (Scripts/*.exe), ignored by git, unusable on Linux. Create your own env from requirements.
- Notebooks use relative paths (`data/...`, `modele/...`) — start Jupyter from the notebook's own directory (`apprentissage/` or `analyse_image/`), or cells fail.
- `analyse_image` requires TensorFlow/Keras (`tensorflow==2.22.0rc0`, `keras-nightly==...`) which is only in its requirements; `apprentissage` uses scikit-learn/joblib/pandas/seaborn.
- `analyse_image/requirements.txt` is UTF-16LE (other text files CRLF); editing with default text tooling will corrupt it.
- No requirements install in devcontainer includes tensorflow? devcontainer installs numpy etc but not tf. Mention maybe.

## C++ architecture
- `main.cpp` — menu/CLI; `Library` owns `unique_ptr<Book/User>`; `FileManager` serializes to pipe-delimited text: Book `title|author|isbn|1/0|borrowerId`, User `name|userId|isbn...` (check user format). Need user.cpp to confirm. Let's check. 
- `Agent` is not an AI agent; directory name only. Important! An agent might assume agent/ is an agent codebase. High signal: "`agent/` is C++ library manager, not an AI agent." The root README calls third part "agent" (AI agent?). Actually project may be about emerging tech and AI; but code is a library manager. It's confusing, worth noting.

- Data files tracked in git; running app with `-d` + exit rewrites them. Warn: don't test with `-d agent/data` because it mutates tracked files / creates .backup files. This is high value! Running the app and choosing save/exit rewrites data/books.txt/users.txt (CRLF → LF too) and `createBackup` creates tracked-adjacent `.backup`. So testing should copy data to /tmp. Great gotcha.

- App auto-saves on exit (case 0) — yes. So any interactive run with -d modifies data. Include.

- `clearScreen` uses `system("cls || clear")` Linux OK.

## Data / model artifacts
- `apprentissage/data/dataset.csv` is generated by `partie1_2.ipynb` from pokedex/combats; tab-separated. Notebook 3 uses column indices 5:12 and target 17 — changing upstream columns breaks training. This is a real fragile coupling worth documenting.
- `analyse_image/classification.ipynb` writes `modele/modele.json` + weights; `test_model.ipynb` loads `modele_performant.*` relative path.
- `apprentissage/partie3_predicitons.ipynb` loads `modele/modele_pokemon.mod`.

## Conventions
- Source comments/UI strings are French; preserve.
- Most tracked text files have CRLF line endings; devcontainer sets `files.eol: \n`. Avoid reformatting whole files (noise). Actually if agent edits, line ending may change from CRLF to LF and git diff whole file. Recommend preserving line endings. But `agent/.gitignore` etc? All CRLF except analyse_image requirements. Could state "preserve existing CRLF line endings in `agent/` and `apprentissage/`; do not run whole-file reformatters." Is that high-signal? It would prevent huge diffs. Yes.

- .vscode/settings.json has `cmake.sourceDirectory` hardcoded to Windows path; don't trust it; CMake source is `agent/`. Could include or omit. It's a gotcha but minor. Might mention under C++ build.

- Devcontainer: open `agent/` as the workspace folder (devcontainer inside agent). Its Dockerfile installs Python ML libs without tensorflow. GUI X11 forwarding. If using, `cmake.configureOnOpen` true. Could say "The devcontainer is scoped to `agent/`; open that folder to use it." The root README says this. Maybe not needed.

- No CI, no test framework.

```markdown
# AGENTS.md

Course assignment (Atelier ML) with three independent parts. No shared package manager, tests, lint, or CI.

## Repo map
- `agent/` — C++17 CLI "bibliotheque" (library manager). Despite the name, it is not an AI agent.
- `apprentissage/` — Jupyter notebooks: Pokémon data prep + scikit-learn regression; artifact `modele/modele_pokemon.mod`.
- `analyse_image/` — Jupyter notebooks: Fashion-MNIST CNN with TensorFlow/Keras; artifacts `modele/*.json` + `*.weights.h5`.
- Each part is run independently; there is no root build/test command.

## C++ (`agent/`)
- Build:
  ```bash
  cd agent
  cmake -S . -B build
  cmake --build build
  ```
  CMake globs `*.cpp` at configure time — re-run cmake after adding/removing source files.
- Run: `./build/bibliotheque -d data` from `agent/`. Without `-d`, `FileManager` has empty paths: no data is loaded or saved.
- `-d` points to a directory containing `books.txt` and `users.txt`; sample data is `agent/data`.
- The program auto-saves on exit (menu `0`). Do not test with `-d data` directly: it rewrites tracked files and `Sauvegarde` creates `*.backup`. Copy data to `/tmp` for testing.
- No tests. Verify by building cleanly (warnings are enabled) and exercising the menu; pipe input for non-interactive runs, e.g. `printf '5\n\n0\n' | ./build/bibliotheque -d /tmp/libdata`.
- Architecture: `main.cpp` menu/loop → `Library` owns `unique_ptr<Book/User>` → `FileManager` loads/saves. Adding a feature touches all three. Raw `Book*`/`User*` from `Library` are non-owning.
- Serialization is line-based pipe-delimited: Book `title|author|isbn|1/0|borrowerId`; User `name|userId|isbn1,isbn2`. Keep this format compatible with `data/*.txt`.
- `.vscode/settings.json` hardcodes a Windows `cmake.sourceDirectory`; ignore it and use `agent/`.

## Python notebooks
- Notebooks use relative paths. Start Jupyter from the notebook's directory:
  `cd apprentissage` or `cd analyse_image`, otherwise `data/...` and `modele/...` fail.
- Cells share state and are order-dependent; validate with "Restart & Run All", not individual cells.
- Dependencies are separate per part; install from that part's `requirements.txt`.
  - `apprentissage`: pandas, scikit-learn, joblib, matplotlib, seaborn.
  - `analyse_image`: TensorFlow/Keras (`tensorflow==2.22.0rc0`, `keras-nightly`), numpy, PIL. The devcontainer does **not** install TensorFlow.
- `analyse_image/.venv/` is a committed? no, ignored Windows venv (`Scripts/*.exe`) and unusable on Linux; don't use it.
- `analyse_image/requirements.txt` is UTF-16LE; decode/re-encode when editing or it will be corrupted.
- `apprentissage/partie1_2.ipynb` generates `data/dataset.csv` (tab-separated). `partie3_entrainement.ipynb` selects columns `5:12`/`17` by position; changing upstream columns silently breaks training.
- `analyse_image/classification.ipynb` writes both model pairs, but its `modele_performant.*` save block uses `reseauNeurone1Convolution` (not the 4-conv `reseauNeurones4Convolution`). `test_model.ipynb` loads `modele_performant.*`.

## Formatting / git
- Tracked text files (C++, README, notebooks? notebooks are JSON CRLF) use CRLF; `analyse_image/requirements.txt` is UTF-16LE. Preserve line endings and encoding; avoid whole-file reformatting.
- Existing docs/UI/comments are in French; keep new user-facing strings French.
- Git worktree currently has unrelated uncommitted changes ... 
```

```markdown
# AGENTS.md

Atelier ML assignment with three independent, unconnected parts. There is no root package, test suite, linter, or CI; each part is run on its own.

## Repo map
- `agent/` — C++17 CLI library manager (`bibliotheque`); not an AI agent despite the name.
- `apprentissage/` — notebooks for Pokémon data prep and scikit-learn regression; model in `modele/modele_pokemon.mod`.
- `analyse_image/` — notebooks for Fashion-MNIST CNN training/inference; model JSON + H5 weights in `modele/`.

## C++ (`agent/`)
- Build (verified):
  ```bash
  cd agent
  cmake -S . -B build
  cmake --build build
  ```
  `CMakeLists.txt` globs `*.cpp` when configuring; re-run CMake after adding/removing sources.
- Run from `agent/`: `./build/bibliotheque -d data`. `-d` must point to a directory containing `books.txt` and `users.txt`.
- Without `-d`, `FileManager` gets empty paths: no data is loaded or saved. Data is only persisted on save/exit.
- Exit (menu `0`) auto-saves. Don't run against `agent/data`: it rewrites tracked data and "Créer une Sauvegarde" writes `*.backup` files. Copy to `/tmp` first:
  ```bash
  cp -r agent/data /tmp/libdata
  printf '0\n' | ./build/bibliotheque -d /tmp/libdata
  ```
- No tests. Verification is a clean build (warnings enabled) plus manual menu runs.
- Flow: `main.cpp` (menu/CLI) → `Library` (owns `unique_ptr<Book/User>`) → `FileManager` (text I/O). Pointers returned by `Library` are non-owning.
- File format is `|`-delimited: Book `title|author|isbn|1/0|borrowerId`; User `name|userId|isbn1,isbn2`. Changes must remain readable by the existing `data/*.txt`.
- The devcontainer is in `agent/.devcontainer`; open `agent/` as the workspace root to use it. It installs Python ML packages but not TensorFlow.

## Python notebooks
- Run Jupyter from the notebook's own directory (`cd apprentissage` or `cd analyse_image`). All paths are relative (`data/...`, `modele/...`).
- Cells share state; validate with Restart & Run All, not by executing one cell.
- Install dependencies separately from each part's `requirements.txt`.
  - `apprentissage/`: pandas, scikit-learn, joblib, matplotlib, seaborn.
  - `analyse_image/`: TensorFlow/Keras (`tensorflow==2.22.0rc0`, `keras-nightly`), numpy, pillow.
- `analyse_image/.venv/` is an ignored Windows virtualenv (`Scripts/*.exe`); it doesn't work on Linux. Create a Linux env instead.
- `analyse_image/requirements.txt` is UTF-16LE (with BOM); use an encoding-aware editor or it will be corrupted.
- `apprentissage/partie1_2.ipynb` generates `data/dataset.csv` (tab-separated). `partie3_entrainement.ipynb` selects features by position (`iloc[:, 5:12]`, target column 17); changing upstream columns silently breaks training.
- `analyse_image/classification.ipynb` saves `modele_performant.*` from `reseauNeurone1Convolution`, not the 4-conv `reseauNeurones4Convolution` trained just above. `test_model.ipynb` consumes `modele_performant.*`.
- `.venv`, and the Fashion-MNIST CSVs under `analyse_image/data/`, are git-ignored but present locally; a fresh clone may not have the CSVs.

## Editing conventions
- `agent/` and `apprentissage/` working files use CRLF; `analyse_image/` notebooks use LF. Preserve existing line endings to avoid whole-file diffs.
- UI strings, comments, and docs are French; keep new user-facing text French.
```

Atelier ML assignment with three independent, unconnected parts. There is no root package, test suite, linter, or CI; each part is run on its own.

## Repo map
- `agent/` — C++17 CLI library manager (`bibliotheque`); not an AI agent despite the name.
- `apprentissage/` — notebooks for Pokémon data prep and scikit-learn regression; model in `modele/modele_pokemon.mod`.

## C++ (`agent/`)
- Build (verified):
  ```bash
  cd agent
  cmake -S . -B build
  cmake --build build
  ```
  `CMakeLists.txt` globs `*.cpp` when configuring; re-run CMake after adding/removing sources.
- Run from `agent/`: `./build/bibliotheque -d data`. `-d` must point to a directory containing `books.txt` and `users.txt`.
- Without `-d`, `FileManager` gets empty paths: no data is loaded or saved. Data is only persisted on save/exit.
- Exit (menu `0`) auto-saves. Don't run against `agent/data`: it rewrites tracked data and “Créer une Sauvegarde” writes `*.backup` files. Copy to `/tmp` first:
  ```bash
  cp -r agent/data /tmp/libdata
  printf '0\n' | ./build/bibliotheque -d /tmp/libdata
  ```
- No tests. Verification is a clean build (warnings enabled) plus manual menu runs.
- Flow: `main.cpp` (menu/CLI) → `Library` (owns `unique_ptr<Book/User>`) → `FileManager` (text I/O). Pointers returned by `Library` are non-owning.
- File format is `|`-delimited: Book `title|author|isbn|1/0|borrowerId`; User `name|userId|isbn1,isbn2`. Changes must remain readable by the existing `data/*.txt`.
- The devcontainer is in `agent/.devcontainer`; open `agent/` as the workspace root to use it. It installs Python ML packages but not TensorFlow.

## Python notebooks
- Run Jupyter from the notebook's own directory (`cd apprentissage` or `cd analyse_image`). All paths are relative (`data/...`, `modele/...`).
- Cells share state; validate with Restart & Run All, not by executing one cell.
- Install dependencies separately from each part's `requirements.txt`.

- `.venv`, and the Fashion-MNIST CSVs under `analyse_image/data/`, are git-ignored but present locally; a fresh clone may not have the CSVs.

## Editing conventions

Design considerations for the pybind11 binding:

**Problem areas:**
1. `Library` holds `vector<unique_ptr<Book>>`. `getAllBooks()` returns raw pointers. Binding with `return_value_policy::reference_internal` to the Library keeps them alive as long as the Library (parent) is alive, and the objects are heap-stable. Good.
2. Python needs to modify Book state? Only through Library methods (`checkOutBook`, `returnBook`) — no direct setters needed, but read-only getters are enough. Exposing setters could break invariants; but `setTitle` etc. are harmless. Keep read-only for UI.
3. UI needs to borrow by borrower *name* (per mockup). Add helper in Python: find user by name → get id → `checkOutBook(isbn, id)`. Could also add `findUserByName` to C++ (new method) — but modifying C++ core is fine and probably better: add `User* findUserByName(const string&)` to `Library`. Hmm, but the task says "create an interface in python while using the c++ code with pybind11", so minimal C++ change is ideal. Python-side helper avoids touching the C++ core. However, for a nice binding, adding a `PyLibrary` wrapper class in `bindings.cpp` that composes `Library` + `FileManager` is the cleanest: e.g. `PyBookRepository` exposing:
   - `load(data_dir) -> bool`
   - `save() -> bool`
   - `books() -> list[dict]` (plain dicts: title, author, isbn, available, borrower_id, borrower_name) — avoids lifetime pitfalls entirely!
   - `users() -> list[dict]`
   - `add_book(title, author, isbn) -> bool`
   - `add_user(name, user_id) -> bool`
   - `checkout(isbn, user_name) -> bool` (or user_id)
   - `return_book(isbn) -> bool`
   - `search(query)` / `filter_books(status)`

Returning plain dicts from the binding is much more robust than exposing raw pointers: the UI never holds stale C++ pointers, and state refresh is trivial. This is a strong recommendation. It also solves the "borrower display name" and "borrow by name" requirements in one place in C++.

Trade-off: less "pure" binding of classes. But we can bind both: bind `Book`/`User` classes (read-only getters) plus a `LibraryService` facade. For a UI, dicts are best.

};
```

Instead of py::dict in C++ (which is fine), use `std::vector<std::map<std::string, ...>>`? Heterogeneous values → use `py::dict`. Simpler: bind Book/User classes with getters, and have Python convert to dicts. That keeps C++ binding idiomatic:

```cpp
py::class_<Book>(m, "Book")
  .def(py::init<const std::string&, const std::string&, const std::string&>())
  .def_property_readonly("title", &Book::getTitle)
  ...
py::class_<User>(m, "User")
  ...
py::class_<Library>(m, "Library")
  .def(py::init<>())
  .def("add_book", &Library::addBook)
  .def("remove_book", &Library::removeBook)
  .def("find_book", &Library::findBookByISBN, py::return_value_policy::reference_internal)
  .def("search_by_title", &Library::searchBooksByTitle, ...)
  .def("search_by_author", ...)
  .def("available_books", &Library::getAvailableBooks, ...)
  .def("all_books", &Library::getAllBooks, ...)
  .def("add_user", &Library::addUser)
  .def("find_user_by_id", ...)
  .def("all_users", ...)
  .def("checkout", &Library::checkOutBook)
  .def("return_book", &Library::returnBook)
  .def_readonly("total_books", &Library::getTotalBooks) // properties
  ...
py::class_<FileManager>(m, "FileManager")
  .def(py::init<const std::string&, const std::string&>())
  .def("load", &FileManager::loadLibraryData)
  .def("save", &FileManager::saveLibraryData)
  .def("backup", &FileManager::createBackup);
```

Then Python wrapper `library_service.py` that holds Library + FileManager, provides dict-based API and name→user resolution. This is clean: pybind11 stays a thin, faithful binding; the Python layer adds convenience. I like this. It also demonstrates pybind11 usage clearly.

No — for pointers in containers, the default return_value_policy is `automatic_reference`? Actually for `std::vector<T*>`, the caster for each element uses `return_value_policy::automatic`? Hmm. `automatic` for pointer types → `take_ownership`, which would delete the Book! That's dangerous. In pybind11, `return_value_policy` applies to the top-level return; for STL containers of pointers, the element policy used is... Let me recall. `list_caster::cast` does:

```cpp

...
handle value = type_caster<Value>::cast(*it, policy, parent);
```
It passes the same `policy` down. So setting `reference` on the method propagates → wrappers are borrows, no ownership. Good. But then Python wrappers become invalid if Library is destroyed. In our app, service lives for app lifetime. Still, safer: use `py::return_value_policy::reference_internal`? For container elements, `reference_internal` adds keep_alive tied to the parent? Alternative: bind `all_books` in a wrapper lambda returning `std::vector<Book>` by value (copies). But `Book` is copyable (has strings, bool). `getAllBooks()` returns pointers; lambda can dereference and copy:
```cpp
.def("all_books", [](Library& lib) {
    std::vector<Book> out;
    for (Book* b : lib.getAllBooks()) out.push_back(*b);
    return out;
})
```
Copies are cheap (few strings) and totally safe. Same for users and searches. Then Python wrappers own their copies; no lifetime issues. Mutations always go through Library by ISBN/ID. This is robust and simple. I'll recommend copy-returning accessors.

But then the UI gets `Book` objects with read-only props and copies. Fine.

**Architecture / router**
`MainWindow(QMainWindow)` with `QStackedWidget`. Pages call back via signals (e.g., `open_book(isbn)`, `open_users()`, `open_add_book()`, `open_add_user()`, `go_home()`, `go_users()`). Pages take `service` in constructor. On `showEvent`/navigation, pages call `reload()` to refresh data.

Simplest router pattern:
```python
class MainWindow:
    def __init__(self, service):
        self.stack = QStackedWidget()
        self.home = HomePage(service)
        ...
        self.home.book_activated.connect(self.open_book)
        self.home.users_requested.connect(self.show_users)
        ...
    def open_book(self, isbn):
        book = self.service.get_book(isbn)
        page = BookDetailPage(service, book)
        page.back_requested.connect(self.show_home)
        page.changed.connect(...)  # navigate to other detail page
```
Page 3→4 and 4→3 transitions: when a book is returned/borrowed, the detail page should re-render as the other variant. Simplest: `open_book(isbn)` re-fetches and creates/updates a single `BookDetailPage` that has two modes. Use one `BookDetailPage` widget with a state flag; after action, call `self.show_book(isbn)` which reloads and switches mode. The spec says after return you go to page 4 — same book's available detail. So a single detail page that re-renders is ideal.

Actually, the two mockups differ substantially (button vs input). Implement as a single `BookDetailPage` with a `QStackedWidget` inside (borrowed view / available view), plus shared header (back, title, author, status). The status + content switches.

**Service layer**
```python
class LibraryService:
    def __init__(self, data_dir: Path):
        self._lib = bibliotheque_py.Library()
        self._fm = bibliotheque_py.FileManager(str(data_dir/"books.txt"), str(data_dir/"users.txt"))
    def load(self) -> bool
    def save(self) -> bool
    def books(self) -> list[Book]          # copies from lib.all_books()
    def users(self) -> list[User]
    def find_user_by_name(self, name) -> User | None   # case-insensitive, trimmed
    def add_book(...) -> tuple[bool, str]  # validates non-empty, duplicate ISBN
    def add_user(...) -> tuple[bool, str]
    def checkout(isbn, user_name) -> tuple[bool, str]
    def return_book(isbn) -> bool
    def find_book(isbn) -> Book | None
```
Return `(ok, message)` so UI shows French errors. Or raise `ValueError`. I'll go with `(ok, message)` — explicit and easy.

**Threading**: no need; operations are instant.

**CMake changes in detail**
Current:
```cmake
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.21)
project(bibliotheque)
file(GLOB SOURCES "*.cpp")
add_executable(${PROJECT_NAME} ${SOURCES})
...
```
New:
```cmake
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.21)
project(bibliotheque LANGUAGES CXX)

set(CORE_SOURCES book.cpp filemanager.cpp library.cpp user.cpp)

add_library(bibliotheque_core STATIC ${CORE_SOURCES})
target_include_directories(bibliotheque_core PUBLIC ${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR})
target_compile_features(bibliotheque_core PUBLIC cxx_std_17)
target_compile_options(bibliotheque_core PRIVATE -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic)

add_executable(bibliotheque main.cpp)
target_link_libraries(bibliotheque PRIVATE bibliotheque_core)
target_compile_options(bibliotheque PRIVATE -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic)

option(BUILD_PYTHON_BINDINGS "Build the pybind11 module" ON)
if(BUILD_PYTHON_BINDINGS)
  find_package(Python3 COMPONENTS Interpreter Development.Module REQUIRED)
  find_package(pybind11 CONFIG REQUIRED)
  pybind11_add_module(bibliotheque_py bindings.cpp)
  target_link_libraries(bibliotheque_py PRIVATE bibliotheque_core)
endif()
```
Note: `CXX_STANDARD 17` on target; C++17 needed for `<filesystem>`. `filemanager.cpp` uses `std::filesystem` — with GCC 14 no extra link needed.

Preserve `CXX_EXTENSIONS NO`.

std::string lower(const std::string& s) { ... } // careful with UTF-8; not needed if filtering in Python
std::vector<Book> copy_books(const std::vector<Book*>& src) { ... }
std::vector<User> copy_users(...) { ... }

std::optional<User> find_user_by_name(Library& lib, const std::string& name) {
   for (User* u : lib.getAllUsers()) if (u->getName() == name) return *u;
   return std::nullopt;
}
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(bibliotheque_py, m) {
  m.doc() = "...";

  py::class_<Book>(m, "Book")
    .def(py::init<>())
    .def(py::init<const std::string&, const std::string&, const std::string&>(),
         py::arg("title"), py::arg("author"), py::arg("isbn"))
    .def_property_readonly("title", &Book::getTitle)
    .def_property_readonly("author", &Book::getAuthor)
    .def_property_readonly("isbn", &Book::getISBN)
    .def_property_readonly("available", &Book::getAvailability)
    .def_property_readonly("borrower_id", &Book::getBorrowerId)
    .def("__repr__", ...);

  py::class_<User>(m, "User")
    .def(py::init<const std::string&, const std::string&>(), ...)
    .def_property_readonly("name", &User::getName)
    .def_property_readonly("user_id", &User::getUserId)
    .def_property_readonly("borrowed_books", &User::getBorrowedBooks)   // stl.h → list[str]
    .def("has_borrowed", &User::hasBorrowedBook)
    .def("__repr__", ...);

  py::class_<Library>(m, "Library")
    .def(py::init<>())
    .def("add_book", &Library::addBook, py::arg("book"))
    .def("remove_book", &Library::removeBook, py::arg("isbn"))
    .def("find_book", &Library::findBookByISBN, py::arg("isbn"), py::return_value_policy::reference_internal)
    .def("all_books", [](Library& l){ return copy_books(l.getAllBooks()); })
    .def("available_books", [](Library& l){ return copy_books(l.getAvailableBooks()); })
    .def("search_by_title", [](Library& l, const std::string& q){ return copy_books(l.searchBooksByTitle(q)); }, ...)
    .def("search_by_author", ...)
    .def("add_user", &Library::addUser)
    .def("find_user", &Library::findUserById, ..., reference_internal)
    .def("find_user_by_name", [](Library& l, const std::string& name) -> py::object { ... })  // or std::optional<User>
    .def("all_users", ...)
    .def("checkout", &Library::checkOutBook, py::arg("isbn"), py::arg("user_id"))
    .def("return_book", &Library::returnBook, py::arg("isbn"))
    .def_property_readonly("total_books", &Library::getTotalBooks)
    .def_property_readonly("available_count", &Library::getAvailableBookCount)
    .def_property_readonly("checked_out_count", &Library::getCheckedOutBookCount);

  py::class_<FileManager>(m, "FileManager")
    .def(py::init<const std::string&, const std::string&>(), py::arg("books_file"), py::arg("users_file"))
    .def("load", &FileManager::loadLibraryData, py::arg("library"))
    .def("save", &FileManager::saveLibraryData, py::arg("library"))
    .def("backup", &FileManager::createBackup)
    .def("file_exists", &FileManager::fileExists, py::arg("filename"));
}
```

Caveat: `find_book`/`find_user` returning raw pointers with `reference_internal`: the parent object is the Library, so lifetime OK. But the UI uses copies; keep them anyway for completeness, or return `std::optional<Book>` copies to avoid all lifetime concerns. I'll return copies via `std::optional<Book>` — consistent. Actually `reference_internal` is instructive for pybind11. But safety first for students; use copies for `find_*` too. Hmm, `find_book` is useful for direct access. Copies are fine since the UI never mutates through Book. I'll make everything copy-based and note it. But then `checkout` etc. are the only mutators. Good.

Also `Library` isn't copyable (unique_ptr members), so `Library` must be held by reference in Python; `py::class_<Library>` with `py::init<>()` creates a Python-owned instance. Fine.

`std::optional<Book>` requires pybind11/stl.h (included) and C++17. Good.

`getAllUsers().size()` etc. available.

Note: `Book` copies are trivially copyable (implicit copy ctor works with strings/bool). Yes.

**Module naming and output**: `pybind11_add_module(bibliotheque_py bindings.cpp)` creates `bibliotheque_py.cpython-313-x86_64-linux-gnu.so` in the build dir. `sys.path.insert(0, build)`.

If `find_package(pybind11)` can't find the pip-installed config: `cmake -S . -B build -Dpybind11_DIR="$(python3 -m pybind11 --cmakedir)"`. Actually pybind11's pip package installs CMake config, and `pip install` places it in site-packages; CMake's `find_package(pybind11 CONFIG)` may not automatically search site-packages. pybind11 docs recommend `-Dpybind11_DIR=$(python3 -m pybind11 --cmakedir)`. I can make CMake do it automatically:

```cmake
if(BUILD_PYTHON_BINDINGS)
  find_package(Python3 COMPONENTS Interpreter Development.Module REQUIRED)
  find_package(pybind11 CONFIG QUIET)
  if(NOT pybind11_FOUND)
    execute_process(
      COMMAND ${Python3_EXECUTABLE} -m pybind11 --cmakedir
      OUTPUT_VARIABLE _pybind11_dir OUTPUT_STRIP_TRAILING_WHITESPACE ERROR_QUIET)
    if(_pybind11_dir)
      list(APPEND CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH "${_pybind11_dir}")
    endif()
    find_package(pybind11 CONFIG REQUIRED)
  endif()
  ...
endif()
```
This auto-discovers the pip install. Nice touch, verified pattern. Good.

I reviewed all seven `maquette/AiReview` pages, the original page notes, and the full C++ API (`Book`, `User`, `Library`, `FileManager`, `main.cpp`). Here's the implementation plan.

## Findings that shape the design

- The mockups define a 7-page desktop flow (book grid → filter → borrowed/available detail → users → add book/user), not the CLI menu.
- Borrowing in the mockup is by **person name**, but `Library::checkOutBook(isbn, userId)` needs a **user ID** → the glue must resolve name → `USRxxx`.
- `Library` owns `unique_ptr<Book/User>` and returns raw pointers. Exposing those directly to Python creates lifetime hazards, so the binding should return **copies**.
- `Library::addBook` / `addUser` do **not** check duplicates (the CLI does it); the Python service must.
- Environment verified: Python 3.13.5 (`/opt/venv`), Python headers present, PyQt5 5.15.11 works offscreen, network works, but **pybind11 is not installed**.
- `agent/data` currently has 41 books / 25 users, 14 of them borrowed (`USR001`–`USR014`).

## Proposed plan

### 1. Stack
- **GUI: PyQt5** — already installed in the devcontainer (`python3-pyqt5` + pip `pyqt5`), X11 forwarding is configured, and QSS reproduces the mockup's rounded cards, colored left borders, dark navy/blue palette far more faithfully than Tkinter.
- **Bindings: thin, faithful pybind11 layer** + a small Python service that adds validation/business rules.

### 2. Build integration (`agent/CMakeLists.txt`)
Split the globbed sources so the binding doesn't get compiled into the CLI:
- `bibliotheque_core` static lib: `book.cpp filemanager.cpp library.cpp user.cpp`, `POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE ON` (required for linking into the `.so`).
- `bibliotheque` executable: `main.cpp` + core.
- `bibliotheque_py` module via `pybind11_add_module` + core.
- Auto-discover pip-installed pybind11 by falling back to `python3 -m pybind11 --cmakedir` when `find_package(pybind11)` fails, so no manual `-Dpybind11_DIR` is needed.
- Keep C++17 and `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic`; preserve the file's CRLF endings.

### 3. `agent/bindings.cpp`
Class bindings with read-only properties and copy-returning list accessors:

```
Book:   title, author, isbn, available, borrower_id  (read-only)
User:   name, user_id, borrowed_books (list[str]), has_borrowed(isbn)
Library:
  add_book(Book), remove_book(isbn),
  find_book(isbn) -> Book|None, all_books(), available_books(),
  search_by_title(q), search_by_author(q),
  add_user(User), find_user(id) -> User|None, find_user_by_name(name) -> User|None,
  all_users(), checkout(isbn, user_id), return_book(isbn),
  total_books, available_count, checked_out_count
FileManager:
  FileManager(books_path, users_path), load(library), save(library), backup(), file_exists(name)
```
`find_*` and list methods return `std::optional<T>` / `std::vector<T>` **by value** so the UI never holds dangling pointers.

### 4. Python layer (`agent/ui/`)
- `service.py` — `LibraryService(data_dir)`: owns `Library` + `FileManager`, validates the directory, auto-saves after every mutation, and returns `(ok, message)` in French. Implements Unicode-aware search (`casefold()` over title/author, because the C++ search uses byte-wise `tolower` and mishandles accents) and name→ID resolution.
- `theme.py` — palette + global QSS (navy `#0F1A2B`, blue `#2563EB`, red `#EF4444`, green `#10B981`, light `#F7F8FA`, rounded pills/cards).
- `widgets.py` — `BookCard` (accent strip, title/author/ISBN, status, borrower), `UserCard`, `PrimaryButton`, `FormField`.
- `pages.py` — `HomePage`, `BookDetailPage`, `UsersPage`, `AddBookPage`, `AddUserPage`.
- `window.py` — `QMainWindow` + `QStackedWidget` router.
- `main.py` — `argparse --data-dir` (default `data`), inserts `<agent>/build` into `sys.path`, starts the app.

### 5. Mockup → code mapping
| Page | Behavior | Calls |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Search (live, title/author), Filtre menu (Aucun/Emprunté/Non emprunté), 3-col card grid, click card | `all_books()` / `available_books()` |
| 2 | Filter dropdown | service-side filter |
| 3 | Borrowed detail, "Par : {name}", Rendre le livre → becomes non-borrowed | `return_book(isbn)` then re-render page 4 |
| 4 | Available detail, name field, Emprunter → validate non-empty + user exists | `find_user_by_name` → `checkout(isbn, id)` → page 3 |
| 5 | User grid (name, `N° id`), back to books | `all_users()` |
| 6 | Add book (3 non-empty fields, duplicate ISBN rejected) → page 1 | `add_book` + save |
| 7 | Add user (2 non-empty fields, duplicate ID rejected) → page 5 | `add_user` + save |

Back buttons: detail → page 1; add book → page 1; add user → page 5; users → page 1.

### 6. Build & run
```bash
cd agent
python3 -m pip install -r requirements-ui.txt        # pybind11, PyQt5
cmake -S . -B build
cmake --build build -j
python3 ui/main.py -d data
```
Also add `pybind11` to the devcontainer's pip list for future rebuilds (optional, since the current container needs the pip install anyway).

### 7. Verification
- `pip install pybind11`, clean rebuild → CLI still builds unchanged, `.so` importable.
- Headless binding test against a **copy** of `agent/data` in `/tmp`: load, list, checkout by name, return, add book/user, save, reload, assert counts.
- `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen` smoke run that instantiates each page and saves screenshots to `/tmp` for comparison with pages 1–7.
- Confirm `git status agent/data` is untouched by tests.

### 8. Gotchas I'll respect
- **Never test against `agent/data`**: the app auto-saves and `FileManager` writes LF, rewriting the tracked CRLF files. Tests use `/tmp`.
- Data persists after every mutation (mockup has no Save button), plus on window close.
- The binding must be rebuilt for the exact interpreter used to run the app.
- `FileManager` prints load/save messages to stdout; acceptable, kept French.
- No core C++ behavior changes beyond adding the binding target.

```cpp
#include <pybind11/pybind11.h>
#include <pybind11/stl.h>

#include <algorithm>
#include <cctype>
#include <optional>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

#include "book.h"
#include "filemanager.h"
#include "library.h"
#include "user.h"

namespace py = pybind11;
using namespace std;

namespace {

vector<Book> copyBooks(const vector<Book*>& books) {
    vector<Book> copies;
    copies.reserve(books.size());
    for (const Book* book : books) {
        if (book) copies.push_back(*book);
    }
    return copies;
}

vector<User> copyUsers(const vector<User*>& users) {
    vector<User> copies;
    copies.reserve(users.size());
    for (const User* user : users) {
        if (user) copies.push_back(*user);
    }
    return copies;
}

optional<Book> findBook(Library& library, const string& isbn) {
    Book* book = library.findBookByISBN(isbn);
    if (!book) return nullopt;
    return *book;
}

optional<User> findUser(Library& library, const string& userId) {
    User* user = library.findUserById(userId);
    if (!user) return nullopt;
    return *user;
}

string trim(const string& value) {
    size_t start = value.find_first_not_of(" \t\r\n");
    if (start == string::npos) return "";
    size_t end = value.find_last_not_of(" \t\r\n");
    return value.substr(start, end - start + 1);
}

string lowerAscii(const string& value) {
    string result = value;
    transform(result.begin(), result.end(), result.begin(),
              [](unsigned char c) { return static_cast<char>(tolower(c)); });
    return result;
}

} // namespace
```

```cpp
string foldFrench(const string& input) {
    string result;
    result.reserve(input.size());
    for (size_t i = 0; i < input.size();) {
        unsigned char c = input[i];
        if (c < 0x80) {
            result.push_back(static_cast<char>(tolower(c)));
            ++i;
            continue;
        }
        // Handle 2-byte UTF-8 uppercase French letters
        if (i + 1 < input.size()) {
            string pair = input.substr(i, 2);
            if (pair == "\xC3\x80") { result += "\xC3\xA0"; i += 2; continue; } // À -> à
            ...
        }
        result.push_back(input[i]);
        ++i;
    }
}
```

There are 2-byte sequences for Latin-1 supplement, plus Œ (C5 92) → œ (C5 93), Æ (C3 86) → æ (C3 A6), Ÿ (C5 B8) → ÿ (C3 BF). Manageable but ~20 cases and error-prone.

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Optional

import bibliotheque_py


class LibraryService:
    def __init__(self, data_dir: Path):
        self.data_dir = Path(data_dir)
        if not self.data_dir.is_dir():
            raise FileNotFoundError(...)
        self._library = bibliotheque_py.Library()
        self._files = bibliotheque_py.FileManager(
            str(self.data_dir / "books.txt"),
            str(self.data_dir / "users.txt"),
        )
        self._files.load(self._library)

    def save(self) -> bool: ...
    def books(self) -> list[Book]: ...
    def users(self) -> list[User]: ...
    def find_book(self, isbn) -> Book | None
    def find_user_by_name(self, name) -> User | None:
        key = name.strip().casefold()
        for user in self.all_users():
            if user.name.strip().casefold() == key:
                return user
        return None

    def add_book(self, title, author, isbn) -> tuple[bool, str]:
        title, author, isbn = title.strip(), author.strip(), isbn.strip()
        if not (title and author and isbn): return False, "Tous les champs sont obligatoires."
        if self._library.find_book(isbn) is not None: return False, f"Un livre avec l'ISBN {isbn} existe déjà."
        self._library.add_book(bibliotheque_py.Book(title, author, isbn))
        if not self.save(): return False, "Le livre a été ajouté, mais la sauvegarde a échoué."
        return True, f"« {title} » a été ajouté."

    def add_user(self, name, user_id) -> tuple[bool, str]: similar
    def checkout(self, isbn, person_name) -> tuple[bool, str]:
        name = person_name.strip()
        if not name: return False, "Veuillez entrer le nom de la personne."
        user = self.find_user_by_name(name)
        if user is None: return False, f"Aucun utilisateur nommé « {name} » n'a été trouvé."
        if not self._library.checkout(isbn, user.user_id): return False, "Impossible d'emprunter ce livre."
        save...
    def return_book(self, isbn) -> tuple[bool,str]
```

#!/usr/bin/env python3
import argparse, sys
from pathlib import Path

AGENT_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
sys.path.insert(0, str(AGENT_DIR / "build"))

from PyQt5.QtWidgets import QApplication, QMessageBox
...
def parse_args():
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=...)
    p.add_argument("-d", "--data-dir", default=str(AGENT_DIR / "data"))
def main():
    args = parse_args()
    app = QApplication(sys.argv)
    app.setApplicationName("Bibliothèque personnelle")
    try:
        service = LibraryService(Path(args.data_dir))
    except FileNotFoundError as exc:
        QMessageBox.critical(None, "Erreur", str(exc)); return 1
    window = MainWindow(service)
    window.show()
    return app.exec_()

```cpp
#include <pybind11/pybind11.h>
#include <pybind11/stl.h>

#include <optional>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

#include "book.h"
#include "filemanager.h"
#include "library.h"
#include "user.h"

namespace py = pybind11;
using namespace std;

namespace {

vector<Book> copyBooks(const vector<Book*>& books) {
    vector<Book> copies;
    copies.reserve(books.size());
    for (const Book* book : books) {
        if (book != nullptr) {
            copies.push_back(*book);
        }
    }
    return copies;
}

vector<User> copyUsers(const vector<User*>& users) {
    vector<User> copies;
    copies.reserve(users.size());
    for (const User* user : users) {
        if (user != nullptr) {
            copies.push_back(*user);
        }
    }
    return copies;
}

optional<Book> findBookCopy(Library& library, const string& isbn) {
    const Book* book = library.findBookByISBN(isbn);
    if (book == nullptr) {
        return nullopt;
    }
    return *book;
}

optional<User> findUserCopy(Library& library, const string& userId) {
    const User* user = library.findUserById(userId);
    if (user == nullptr) {
        return nullopt;
    }
    return *user;
}

}  // namespace

PYBIND11_MODULE(bibliotheque_py, m) {
    m.doc() = "Bindings Python du gestionnaire de bibliothèque C++.";

    py::class_<Book>(m, "Book", "Livre de la bibliothèque.")
        .def(py::init<>(), "Crée un livre vide.")
        .def(py::init<const string&, const string&, const string&>(),
             py::arg("title"), py::arg("author"), py::arg("isbn"),
             "Crée un livre disponible.")
        .def_property_readonly("title", &Book::getTitle)
        .def_property_readonly("author", &Book::getAuthor)
        .def_property_readonly("isbn", &Book::getISBN)
        .def_property_readonly("available", &Book::getAvailability)
        .def_property_readonly("borrower_id", &Book::getBorrowerId)
        .def("__repr__", [](const Book& book) {
            return "<Book " + book.getTitle() + " (" + book.getISBN() + ")>";
        });

    py::class_<User>(m, "User", "Utilisateur de la bibliothèque.")
        .def(py::init<>(), "Crée un utilisateur vide.")
        .def(py::init<const string&, const string&>(),
             py::arg("name"), py::arg("user_id"))
        .def_property_readonly("name", &User::getName)
        .def_property_readonly("user_id", &User::getUserId)
        .def_property_readonly("borrowed_books", &User::getBorrowedBooks)
        .def("has_borrowed", &User::hasBorrowedBook, py::arg("isbn"))
        .def("__repr__", [](const User& user) {
            return "<User " + user.getName() + " (" + user.getUserId() + ")>";
        });

    py::class_<Library>(m, "Library", "Collection de livres et d'utilisateurs.")
        .def(py::init<>())
        .def("add_book", &Library::addBook, py::arg("book"))
        .def("remove_book", &Library::removeBook, py::arg("isbn"))
        .def("find_book", &findBookCopy, py::arg("isbn"))
        .def("all_books", [](Library& library) { return copyBooks(library.getAllBooks()); })
        .def("available_books", [](Library& library) { return copyBooks(library.getAvailableBooks()); })
        .def("search_by_title", [](Library& library, const string& title) {
            return copyBooks(library.searchBooksByTitle(title));
        }, py::arg("title"))
        .def("search_by_author", [](Library& library, const string& author) {
            return copyBooks(library.searchBooksByAuthor(author));
        }, py::arg("author"))
        .def("add_user", &Library::addUser, py::arg("user"))
        .def("find_user", &findUserCopy, py::arg("user_id"))
        .def("all_users", [](Library& library) { return copyUsers(library.getAllUsers()); })
        .def("checkout", &Library::checkOutBook, py::arg("isbn"), py::arg("user_id"))
        .def("return_book", &Library::returnBook, py::arg("isbn"))
        .def_property_readonly("total_books", &Library::getTotalBooks)
        .def_property_readonly("available_count", &Library::getAvailableBookCount)
        .def_property_readonly("checked_out_count", &Library::getCheckedOutBookCount);

    py::class_<FileManager>(m, "FileManager", "Lecture et écriture des fichiers de données.")
        .def(py::init<const string&, const string&>(),
             py::arg("books_file"), py::arg("users_file"))
        .def("load", &FileManager::loadLibraryData, py::arg("library"))
        .def("save", &FileManager::saveLibraryData, py::arg("library"))
        .def("backup", &FileManager::createBackup)
        .def("file_exists", &FileManager::fileExists, py::arg("filename"));
}
```

`getAllBooks` etc. are non-const, taking `Library&`. Fine.

`std::optional<Book>` with pybind11/stl.h requires `py::detail::type_caster<std::optional<T>>`; included.

CMakeLists: let me write it. Need to preserve CRLF.

```cmake
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.21)
project(bibliotheque LANGUAGES CXX)

# Sources du coeur de l'application (sans main.cpp ni bindings.cpp)
set(CORE_SOURCES
    book.cpp
    filemanager.cpp
    library.cpp
    user.cpp
)

add_library(bibliotheque_core STATIC ${CORE_SOURCES})
target_include_directories(bibliotheque_core PUBLIC ${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR})

# Specifie qu'on veut la version C++17 du langage
set_target_properties(bibliotheque_core PROPERTIES
    CXX_STANDARD 17
    CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED YES
    CXX_EXTENSIONS NO
    POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE ON
)

# flag pour tous les warnings possible
target_compile_options(bibliotheque_core PRIVATE -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic)

add_executable(${PROJECT_NAME} main.cpp)
target_link_libraries(${PROJECT_NAME} PRIVATE bibliotheque_core)
target_compile_options(${PROJECT_NAME} PRIVATE -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic)

# Module Python (pybind11)
option(BUILD_PYTHON_BINDINGS "Compile le module Python bibliotheque_py" ON)

if(BUILD_PYTHON_BINDINGS)
    find_package(Python3 COMPONENTS Interpreter Development.Module REQUIRED)
    find_package(pybind11 CONFIG QUIET)

    if(NOT pybind11_FOUND)
        execute_process(
            COMMAND ${Python3_EXECUTABLE} -m pybind11 --cmakedir
            OUTPUT_VARIABLE PYBIND11_CMAKE_DIR
            OUTPUT_STRIP_TRAILING_WHITESPACE
            ERROR_QUIET
        )
        if(PYBIND11_CMAKE_DIR)
            list(APPEND CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH "${PYBIND11_CMAKE_DIR}")
        endif()
        find_package(pybind11 CONFIG REQUIRED)
    endif()

    pybind11_add_module(bibliotheque_py bindings.cpp)
    target_link_libraries(bibliotheque_py PRIVATE bibliotheque_core)
endif()
```

`project(bibliotheque LANGUAGES CXX)` — original `project(bibliotheque)` implies C and CXX; explicit CXX is fine.

Warning: `target_compile_options` with `-Wall` on MSVC would fail, but the repo is Linux/devcontainer. Original used the same flags unconditionally. Keep.

Note: The original executable target name `${PROJECT_NAME}` = bibliotheque. Good.

The module output name is `bibliotheque_py`.

Also: `pybind11_add_module` sets C++ standard automatically to 17? pybind11 3 requires C++17 and sets it. To be safe, set:
```cmake
target_compile_features(bibliotheque_py PRIVATE cxx_std_17)
```
pybind11_add_module already uses `pybind11::module` which has `cxx_std_17` INTERFACE for v3. Fine either way; add explicit for clarity? Keep it minimal.

"""Palette et feuille de style de l'interface."""

from string import Template

NAVY = "#0F1A2B"
NAVY_SOFT = "#172438"
SURFACE = "#F4F6FA"
CARD = "#FFFFFF"
BLUE = "#2563EB"
BLUE_DARK = "#1D4ED8"
TEXT = "#F8FAFC"
TEXT_MUTED = "#94A3B8"
TEXT_DARK = "#0F172A"
TEXT_DARK_MUTED = "#64748B"
RED = "#EF4444"
GREEN = "#10B981"
FIELD = "#1F2A3C"
FIELD_BORDER = "#2C3A52"

_QSS = Template("""
QWidget {
    font-family: "Inter", "Segoe UI", "DejaVu Sans", sans-serif;
    font-size: 14px;
    color: $text;
}

QMainWindow, QWidget#Root, QStackedWidget#Stack {
    background-color: $navy;
}

QLabel { background: transparent; }

QPushButton {
    border: none;
    border-radius: 18px;
    padding: 9px 20px;
    font-weight: 600;
    color: #FFFFFF;
    background-color: $blue;
}
QPushButton:hover { background-color: $blue_dark; }
QPushButton:pressed { background-color: #1E40AF; }
QPushButton:disabled { background-color: #334155; color: #94A3B8; }

QPushButton#GhostButton {
    background-color: $blue;
    padding: 8px 18px;
}

QPushButton#FilterButton {
    background-color: $navy_soft;
    color: $text;
    border: 1px solid $field_border;
}
QPushButton#FilterButton:hover { background-color: #22334D; }

QLineEdit {
    background-color: $field;
    border: 1px solid $field_border;
    border-radius: 10px;
    padding: 12px 14px;
    color: $text;
    selection-background-color: $blue;
}
QLineEdit:focus { border: 1px solid $blue; }

QFrame#BookCard, QFrame#UserCard {
    background-color: $card;
    border-radius: 12px;
}
QFrame#UserCard { background-color: #E9EDF4; }  // hmm mockup user cards are very light blue-gray

QFrame#Panel {
    background-color: $surface;
    border-radius: 16px;
    color: $text_dark;
}

QLabel#PageTitle { font-size: 26px; font-weight: 700; color: $text_dark; }
QLabel#CardTitle { font-size: 15px; font-weight: 700; color: $text_dark; }
QLabel#CardMeta { font-size: 12px; color: $text_dark_muted; }
QLabel#StatusBorrowed { font-size: 13px; font-weight: 600; color: $red; }
QLabel#StatusAvailable { font-size: 13px; font-weight: 600; color: $green; }
QLabel#BorrowerName { font-size: 13px; color: $text_dark_muted; }
QLabel#DetailTitle { font-size: 30px; font-weight: 700; color: $text_dark; }
QLabel#DetailAuthor { font-size: 16px; color: $text_dark_muted; }
QLabel#DetailStatusBorrowed { font-size: 17px; font-weight: 700; color: $red; }
QLabel#DetailStatusAvailable { font-size: 17px; font-weight: 700; color: $green; }
QLabel#DetailText { font-size: 15px; color: $text_dark; }
QLabel#ErrorLabel { font-size: 13px; color: $red; }
QLabel#EmptyLabel { font-size: 15px; color: $text_muted; }
QLabel#UserCardName { font-size: 15px; font-weight: 700; color: $text_dark; }
QLabel#UserCardId { font-size: 13px; color: $text_dark_muted; }

QScrollArea { border: none; background: transparent; }
QScrollArea > QWidget > QWidget { background: transparent; }
QScrollBar:vertical { background: transparent; width: 10px; margin: 0; }
QScrollBar::handle:vertical { background: #334155; border-radius: 5px; min-height: 30px; }
QScrollBar::add-line:vertical, QScrollBar::sub-line:vertical { height: 0; }

QMenu {
    background-color: $navy_soft;
    border: 1px solid $field_border;
    border-radius: 10px;
    padding: 6px;
    color: $text;
}
QMenu::item {
    padding: 9px 26px 9px 14px;
    border-radius: 6px;
}
QMenu::item:selected { background-color: $blue; }
QMenu::indicator { width: 0; }
""")

STYLESHEET = _QSS.substitute(
    navy=NAVY, navy_soft=NAVY_SOFT, surface=SURFACE, card=CARD, blue=BLUE,
    blue_dark=BLUE_DARK, text=TEXT, text_muted=TEXT_MUTED, text_dark=TEXT_DARK,

)

### widgets.py

```python
from PyQt5.QtCore import Qt, pyqtSignal
from PyQt5.QtGui import QColor, QPalette
from PyQt5.QtWidgets import (QFrame, QLabel, QLineEdit, QPushButton,
                             QHBoxLayout, QVBoxLayout, QSizePolicy)

from theme import BLUE, GREEN, RED, TEXT_MUTED


def set_placeholder_color(line_edit, color=TEXT_MUTED):
    palette = line_edit.palette()
    palette.setColor(QPalette.PlaceholderText, QColor(color))
    line_edit.setPalette(palette)


def primary_button(text: str) -> QPushButton:
    button = QPushButton(text)
    button.setCursor(Qt.PointingHandCursor)
    return button


class BookCard(QFrame):
    activated = pyqtSignal(str)

    def __init__(self, book, parent=None):
        super().__init__(parent)
        self._isbn = book.isbn
        self.setObjectName("BookCard")
        self.setCursor(Qt.PointingHandCursor)
        self.setSizePolicy(QSizePolicy.Expanding, QSizePolicy.Preferred)
        self.setMinimumHeight(96)

        accent_color = RED if not book.available else GREEN
        accent = QFrame()
        accent.setFixedWidth(6)
        accent.setStyleSheet(
            f"background-color: {accent_color};"
            "border-top-left-radius: 12px; border-bottom-left-radius: 12px;"
        )

        title = QLabel(book.title)
        title.setObjectName("CardTitle")
        title.setWordWrap(False)
        author = QLabel(book.author)
        author.setObjectName("CardMeta")
        isbn = QLabel(book.isbn)
        isbn.setObjectName("CardMeta")

        left = QVBoxLayout()
        left.setSpacing(4)
        left.addWidget(title)
        left.addWidget(author)
        left.addWidget(isbn)
        left.addStretch()

        status = QLabel("Non emprunté" if book.available else "Emprunté")
        status.setObjectName("StatusAvailable" if book.available else "StatusBorrowed")
        status.setAlignment(Qt.AlignRight | Qt.AlignTop)

        borrower = QLabel("")
        borrower.setObjectName("BorrowerName")
        borrower.setAlignment(Qt.AlignRight | Qt.AlignBottom)

        right = QVBoxLayout()
        right.setSpacing(4)
        right.addWidget(status)
        right.addWidget(borrower)

        content = QHBoxLayout()
        content.setContentsMargins(16, 12, 16, 12)
        content.setSpacing(12)
        content.addLayout(left, 1)
        content.addLayout(right, 0)

        root = QHBoxLayout(self)
        root.setContentsMargins(0, 0, 0, 0)
        root.setSpacing(0)
        root.addWidget(accent)
        root.addLayout(content, 1)

        self._borrower_label = borrower

    def set_borrower_name(self, name):
        self._borrower_label.setText(name)

    def mouseReleaseEvent(self, event):
        if event.button() == Qt.LeftButton and self.rect().contains(event.pos()):
            self.activated.emit(self._isbn)
        super().mouseReleaseEvent(event)

HomePage:

```python
class HomePage(QWidget):
    book_activated = pyqtSignal(str)
    users_requested = pyqtSignal()
    add_book_requested = pyqtSignal()

    def __init__(self, service, parent=None):
        super().__init__(parent)
        self._service = service
        self._filter = "all"
        self._cards = []

        self.users_button = primary_button("Utilisateur →")
        self.add_button = primary_button("Ajouter un livre")
        self.search = QLineEdit()
        self.search.setPlaceholderText("Recherche : titre ou auteur")
        set_placeholder_color(self.search)
        self.filter_button = QPushButton("Filtre")
        self.filter_button.setObjectName("FilterButton")
        self.filter_button.setFixedWidth(150)
        self.filter_button.setCursor(Qt.PointingHandCursor)

        self.filter_menu = QMenu(self)
        group = QActionGroup(self.filter_menu)
        group.setExclusive(True)
        for key, label in (("all", "Aucun"), ("borrowed", "Emprunté"), ("available", "Non emprunté")):
            action = QAction(label, self.filter_menu, checkable=True)
            action.setData(key)
            action.setChecked(key == "all")
            group.addAction(action)
            self.filter_menu.addAction(action)
        self.filter_menu.triggered.connect(self._on_filter_selected)

        header = QHBoxLayout()
        header.addWidget(self.users_button)
        header.addStretch(1)
        header.addWidget(self.add_button)

        search_row = QHBoxLayout()
        search_row.setSpacing(12)
        search_row.addWidget(self.search, 1)
        search_row.addWidget(self.filter_button)

        self.grid_host = QWidget()
        self.grid = QGridLayout(self.grid_host)
        self.grid.setContentsMargins(0, 0, 0, 0)
        self.grid.setHorizontalSpacing(16)
        self.grid.setVerticalSpacing(16)
        for column in range(3):
            self.grid.setColumnStretch(column, 1)

        self.scroll = QScrollArea()
        self.scroll.setWidgetResizable(True)
        self.scroll.setWidget(self.grid_host)
        self.scroll.setHorizontalScrollBarPolicy(Qt.ScrollBarAlwaysOff)
        self.scroll.viewport().setStyleSheet("background: transparent;")
        self.grid_host.setStyleSheet("background: transparent;")

        root = QVBoxLayout(self)
        root.setContentsMargins(24, 22, 24, 24)
        root.setSpacing(16)
        root.addLayout(header)
        root.addLayout(search_row)
        root.addWidget(self.scroll, 1)

        self.search.textChanged.connect(self.refresh)
        self.filter_button.clicked.connect(self._show_filter_menu)
        self.users_button.clicked.connect(self.users_requested)
        self.add_button.clicked.connect(self.add_book_requested)

    def _show_filter_menu(self):
        self.filter_menu.exec_(self.filter_button.mapToGlobal(QPoint(0, self.filter_button.height() + 6)))

    def _on_filter_selected(self, action):
        self._filter = action.data()
        self.refresh()

    def reload(self):
        self.refresh()

    def refresh(self):
        while self.grid.count():
            item = self.grid.takeAt(0)
            widget = item.widget()
            if widget is not None:
                widget.deleteLater()

        query = self.search.text().strip().casefold()
        books = []
        for book in self._service.books():
            if query and query not in book.title.casefold() and query not in book.author.casefold():
                continue
            if self._filter == "borrowed" and book.available:
                continue
            if self._filter == "available" and not book.available:
                continue
            books.append(book)

        if not books:
            empty = QLabel("Aucun livre ne correspond à cet affichage.")
            empty.setObjectName("EmptyLabel")
            empty.setAlignment(Qt.AlignCenter)
            self.grid.addWidget(empty, 0, 0, 1, 3)
            return

        for index, book in enumerate(books):
            card = BookCard(book)
            if not book.available:
                borrower = self._service.find_user_by_id(book.borrower_id)
                card.set_borrower_name(borrower.name if borrower else book.borrower_id)
            card.activated.connect(self.book_activated)
            self.grid.addWidget(card, index // 3, index % 3)

        self.grid.setRowStretch(len(books) // 3 + 1, 1)
```

Issue: after clearing, previous row stretches accumulate. `setRowStretch` for arbitrary rows; reset them. Simpler: add a stretch spacer at the bottom? Use a container with a VBox? Let's manage: before adding, clear stretches for rows 0..max. Since we delete items, row stretch settings persist. Track `self._last_rows` and set stretch 0. Or simpler: `self.grid.addWidget(card, ...)` and then at the end `self.grid.setRowStretch(row_count, 1)`; old stretch on a previous row index could cause a gap. To avoid, reset all stretches each refresh:

```python
for row in range(self.grid.rowCount()):
    self.grid.setRowStretch(row, 0)
```
before clearing. Then set final stretch. Good.

Also when empty, grid row 0 has the label and no stretch; add `self.grid.setRowStretch(1, 1)` to top-align. Fine.

Load:
```python
def load(self, isbn):
    book = self._service.find_book(isbn)
    if book is None:
        self.back_requested.emit()
        return
    self._isbn = isbn
    self.title.setText(book.title)
    self.author.setText(book.author)
    self.error.hide()
    if book.available:
        self.borrowed_view.hide()
        self.available_view.show()
        self.name_input.clear()
        self.name_input.setFocus()
    else:
        self.available_view.hide()
        self.borrowed_view.show()
        borrower = self._service.find_user_by_id(book.borrower_id)
        name = borrower.name if borrower else (book.borrower_id or "inconnu")
        self.borrower.setText(f"Par : {name}")
```
Careful: `self.name_input.setFocus()` when hidden? Only available. Good.

main.py:
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Point d'entrée de l'interface graphique de la bibliothèque."""

import argparse
import sys
from pathlib import Path

AGENT_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
BUILD_DIR = AGENT_DIR / "build"

# Le module compilé par pybind11 se trouve dans le répertoire de compilation.
sys.path.insert(0, str(BUILD_DIR))
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))

from PyQt5.QtWidgets import QApplication, QMessageBox  # noqa: E402

from service import LibraryService  # noqa: E402
from theme import STYLESHEET  # noqa: E402
from window import MainWindow  # noqa: E402


def parse_arguments():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Interface graphique de gestion de bibliothèque.")
    parser.add_argument("-d", "--data-dir", default=str(AGENT_DIR / "data"),
                        help="Répertoire contenant books.txt et users.txt (défaut : agent/data).")
    return parser.parse_args()


def main() -> int:
    args = parse_arguments()
    app = QApplication(sys.argv)
    app.setApplicationName("Bibliothèque personnelle")
    app.setStyleSheet(STYLESHEET)

    try:
        service = LibraryService(Path(args.data_dir))
    except (FileNotFoundError, ImportError) as error:
        QMessageBox.critical(None, "Erreur de démarrage", str(error))
        return 1

    window = MainWindow(service)
    window.show()
    return app.exec_()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

ImportError happens at module import time (`from service import`), before main. If bibliotheque_py is missing, `service` import raises ImportError and the script crashes with a traceback. To show a friendly message, wrap the import in try/except at top and store the error:

```python
try:
    from service import LibraryService
except ImportError as exc:
    LIBRARY_IMPORT_ERROR = exc
```
Then in main, after QApplication, check. But `theme`/`window` imports also pull PyQt, which exists. Keep it simple: top-level try around service import:

```python
_service_error = None
try:
    from service import LibraryService
except ImportError as exc:
    _service_error = exc
    LibraryService = None
```
Then in main:
```python
if _service_error is not None:
    QMessageBox.critical(None, "Module manquant",
        "Le module Python « bibliotheque_py » est introuvable.\n"
        "Compilez-le avec :\n  cmake -S . -B build && cmake --build build\n\n"
        f"Détail : {_service_error}")
    return 1
```
Good.

Import order with `sys.path` insert before imports. Since `main.py` lives in ui/, `sys.path[0]` is already ui/ when run as a script, but adding it explicitly makes it work when imported.

The build dir path: `agent/build`. The module is generated in `build/`. Good.

service.py:

```python
"""Couche applicative autour des classes C++ exposées par pybind11."""

from pathlib import Path

import bibliotheque_py


class LibraryService:
    def __init__(self, data_dir: Path):
        self.data_dir = Path(data_dir)
        if not self.data_dir.is_dir():
            raise FileNotFoundError(
                f"Le répertoire de données « {self.data_dir} » n'existe pas."
            )
        self._library = bibliotheque_py.Library()
        self._files = bibliotheque_py.FileManager(
            str(self.data_dir / "books.txt"),
            str(self.data_dir / "users.txt"),
        )
        self._files.load(self._library)

    # --- lecture ---
    def books(self):
        return self._library.all_books()

    def users(self):
        return self._library.all_users()

    def find_book(self, isbn: str):
        return self._library.find_book(isbn)

    def find_user_by_id(self, user_id: str):
        return self._library.find_user(user_id)

    def find_user_by_name(self, name: str):
        key = name.strip().casefold()
        if not key:
            return None
        for user in self.users():
            if user.name.strip().casefold() == key:
                return user
        return None

    # --- écriture ---
    def save(self) -> bool:
        return self._files.save(self._library)

    def add_book(self, title, author, isbn):
        title = title.strip(); ...
        if not title or not author or not isbn:
            return False, "Tous les champs sont obligatoires."
        if self.find_book(isbn) is not None:
            return False, f"Un livre avec l'ISBN « {isbn} » existe déjà."
        self._library.add_book(bibliotheque_py.Book(title, author, isbn))
        if not self.save():
            return False, "Le livre a été ajouté, mais la sauvegarde a échoué."
        return True, ""

    def add_user(self, name, user_id):
        ...
        if self.find_user_by_id(user_id) is not None: ...
        self._library.add_user(bibliotheque_py.User(name, user_id))
        ...

    def checkout(self, isbn, person_name):
        name = person_name.strip()
        if not name:
            return False, "Veuillez entrer le nom de la personne."
        user = self.find_user_by_name(name)
        if user is None:
            return False, f"Aucun utilisateur nommé « {name} » n'a été trouvé."
        if not self._library.checkout(isbn, user.user_id):
            return False, "Impossible d'emprunter ce livre."
        if not self.save():
            return False, "L'emprunt a été enregistré, mais la sauvegarde a échoué."
        return True, ""

    def return_book(self, isbn):
        if not self._library.return_book(isbn):
            return False, "Impossible de retourner ce livre."
        if not self.save():
            return False, "Le retour a été enregistré, mais la sauvegarde a échoué."
        return True, ""
```

Note: `add_book` failure on save still leaves it in memory; message clarifies. Acceptable.

return None\n\n        for user in self.users():\n            if user.name.strip().casefold() == key:\n                return user\n\n        return None\n\n    # --- Écriture Navigation depuis l'accueil.\n        self.home_page.book_activated.connect(self.show_book)\n        self.home_page.users_requested.connect(self.show_users)\n        self.home_page.add_book_requested.connect(self.show_add_book)\n\n        #   "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Point d'entrée de l'interface graphique de la bibliothèque.\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nAGENT_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent\nBUILD_DIR = AGENT_DIR / \"build\"\nUI_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent\n\n# Le module compilé par pybind11 est généré dans le répertoire de compilation.\nsys.path.insert(0, str(BUILD_DIR))\nsys.path.insert(0, str(UI_DIR))\n\nfrom PyQt5.QtWidgets import QApplication, QMessageBox  # noqa: E402\n\n_service_error = None\n\ntry:\n    from service import LibraryService  # noqa: E402\nexcept ImportError as error:  # module pybind11 non compilé ou introuvable\n    _service_error = error\n    LibraryService = None\n\nfrom theme import STYLESHEET  # noqa: E402\nfrom window import MainWindow  # noqa: E402\n\n\ndef parse_arguments():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        description=\"Interface graphique de gestion de bibliothèque personnelle.\"\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"-d\",\n        \"--data-dir\",\n        default=str(AGENT_DIR / \"data\"),\n        help=\"Répertoire contenant books.txt et users.txt (défaut : agent/data).\",\n    )\n    return parser.parse_args()\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    args = parse_arguments()\n\n    app = QApplication(sys.argv)\n    app.setApplicationName(\"Bibliothèque personnelle\")\n    app.setStyleSheet(STYLESHEET)\n\n    if _service_error is not None:\n        QMessageBox.critical(\n            None,\n            \"Module Python manquant\",\n            \"Le module « bibliotheque_py » est introuvable.\\n\"\n            \"Compilez-le avec :\\n\\n\"\n            \"  cmake -S . -B build\\n\"\n            \"  cmake --build build\\n\\n\"\n            f\"Détail : {_service_error}\",\n        )\n        return 1\n\n    try:\n        service = LibraryService(Path(args.data_dir))\n    except FileNotFoundError as error:\n        QMessageBox.critical(None, \"Erreur de démarrage\", str(error))\n        return 1\n\n    window = MainWindow(service)\n    window.show()\n\n    return app.exec_()\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n",

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

Two things:
1. `book.borrower_id` printed `'\r'` for an available book! That means the data file has CRLF line endings, and `fromFileFormat` reads the trailing `\r` into `borrowerId` after the last `|`. For available books: `...|1|` then `\r` remains → borrowerId = "\r". For borrowed books, borrowerId = "USR001\r"? Let's see: line `...|0|USR001\r` → borrowerId becomes "USR001\r". That's a data parsing quirk due to CRLF. The UI would display "Par : Marie Dubois" via find_user_by_id("USR001\r") → fails → shows "USR001\r" (with a stray CR, visually invisible but wrong).

Also `User::fromFileFormat` last field `booksStr` for users with no books: `Alexandra Dumont|USR015|\r` → getline(ss, booksStr, '|') reads until '|' → booksStr empty? Let's trace: line "Alexandra Dumont|USR015|\r". getline(name,'|') → "Alexandra Dumont"; getline(userId,'|') → "USR015"; getline(booksStr,'|') → everything until next '|' or EOF = "\r". So booksStr = "\r", then split by ',' yields "\r" → borrowedBooks contains "\r"! That's a bug for users with no books, and for users with books: "USR001|9782...\r" → last ISBN includes "\r". So `hasBorrowedBook` comparisons fail for returns (but `return_book` loops users and uses `hasBorrowedBook(isbn)` — with "\r" it won't match, so the user's borrowed list won't be updated!). Wait, in our test, after checkout the in-memory User has the exact ISBN (no \r), and return works because the in-memory list has the correct value. But after a save+reload, the user's borrowed books have "\r" appended, so returning a book would not remove it from the user's list (though `Book::returnBook` still works). That's a pre-existing bug exposed by CRLF data.

The original data files in the repo have CRLF in the working tree (tracked index LF). The CLI presumably has this bug too when run on Windows/CRLF. But our app should handle it robustly.

Fix options:
- In `Book::fromFileFormat` and `User::fromFileFormat`, strip trailing `\r`. That's a core C++ change, small and clearly correct (handles CRLF files). The plan said no core behavior changes beyond bindings, but this is a genuine bug fix required for the interface to work. I should explain it. It's minimal: trim trailing '\r' from the line before parsing, or strip '\r' from parsed fields.

Best: in `fromFileFormat`, after parsing, strip a trailing `\r` from the last fields. Simpler: at the top of fromFileFormat, do `string line = rawLine; if (!line.empty() && line.back() == '\r') line.pop_back();` — but the parameter is `const string& line`; create a local copy.

Book:
```cpp
void Book::fromFileFormat(const string& line) {
    string cleanLine = line;
    if (!cleanLine.empty() && cleanLine.back() == '\r') {
        cleanLine.pop_back();
    }
    stringstream ss(cleanLine);
    ...
}
```
Careful: the name `line` shadows; use `cleanLine`.

User: same.

This fixes borrower_id "\r" and the borrowed list "\r". It also makes the CLI robust. Justified.

Alternatively handle in bindings.cpp by normalizing, but the C++ load path is the source. Fixing the core is better and affects both CLI and Python identically.

```
Replace with:
```cpp
// Parse from file format
void Book::fromFileFormat(const string& line) {
    // Les fichiers peuvent utiliser des fins de ligne Windows (CRLF).
    string cleanLine = line;
    if (!cleanLine.empty() && cleanLine.back() == '\r') {
        cleanLine.pop_back();
    }

    stringstream ss(cleanLine);
    string token;
```

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

There's a serious rendering bug in the filter screenshot: ghost/leftover cards from the previous layout (e.g., "Le Cid" appears twice, "Tartuffe/Dom Juan/Andromaque" from the unfiltered list at the bottom, overlapping cards). This is because `_clear_layout` calls `widget.deleteLater()`, which defers deletion until the event loop processes events. When refresh is called multiple times in quick succession (trigger → refresh), old widgets remain visible until deleteLater is processed. In the real app, the event loop runs between user actions, so this might resolve. But `app.processEvents()` should process deferred deletion... Apparently `deleteLater` events are only processed with `sendPostedEvents(None, QEvent.DeferredDelete)`, and `processEvents` may not include DeferredDelete (Qt docs: deferred deletes are processed when returning to the event loop, and processEvents doesn't process them unless explicitly). So the ghosting is a test artifact, but it could also occur briefly in the real app between refreshes (e.g., typing in the search field quickly) — old widgets would persist until the event loop returns to idle. Since `refresh()` is called on every keystroke, old cards may linger/overlap briefly. That's a real issue.

Fix: in `_clear_layout`, call `widget.setParent(None)` (immediately removes from the layout and hides it) and `widget.deleteLater()`. Setting the parent to None immediately removes it from display. Or call `widget.hide()` before deleteLater. The cleanest: 
```python
widget.setParent(None)
widget.deleteLater()
```
This immediately detaches the widget so it won't paint. Common pattern.

Let me apply that.

Also note the duplicate "Le Cid" in the middle: the first grid's cards remained. Yes.

After the fix, re-run the smoke test and inspect.

Also, the filter screenshot shows 14 borrowed cards, some ghosting. After fix it should show exactly 14.

Let me update `_clear_layout`.

{
  "newString": "def _clear_layout(layout) -> None:\n    while layout.count():\n        item = layout.takeAt(0)\n        widget = item.widget()\n        if widget is not None:\n            # setParent(None) retire immédiatement le widget de l'affichage ;\n            # deleteLater() libère la mémoire au prochain tour de boucle.\n            widget.setParent(None)\n            widget.deleteLater()",
  "oldString": "def _clear_layout(layout) -> None:\n    while layout.count():\n        item = layout.takeAt(0)\n        widget = item.widget()\n        if widget is not None:\n            widget.deleteLater()",
  "path": "/workspaces/420-5G4-ML/agent/ui/pages.py"
}

# Interface graphique (Python)

L'interface utilise PyQt5 et appelle le code C++ via pybind11.
Installez les dépendances puis compilez le module Python :

```
$ python3 -m pip install -r requirements-ui.txt
$ cmake -S . -B build
$ cmake --build build
```

Lancez l'interface depuis le répertoire `agent/` :

```
$ python3 ui/main.py -d data
```

Le répertoire passé à `-d` doit contenir `books.txt` et `users.txt`.
